#5170. Why Is XRP Down Today? Three Reasons According to Onchain Data

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

XRP price saw a sell-off over the weekend, with liquidations accelerating in the last 24 hours. At the time of writing, XRP was down 3.43% in the last 24 hours to $0.969 and down 17% weekly.

Onchain data platform CoinGlass seeks to answer why XRP's price is down. Its analysis, generated from the past 24 hours of futures, spot and ETF data, points to three indicators that help explain the pressure on XRP.

First is a 'long squeeze,' which increased selling pressure on the price. Long traders have taken the biggest hit during the latest decline. CoinGlass data shows that XRP long liquidations reached $43.17 million over the past 24 hours, accounting for about 91% of total liquidations.

Second, open interest has fallen as traders, largely longs, close positions. XRP open interest has fallen 5.53% in the past 24 hours to $2.03 billion.

Thirdly, XRP is currently in negative funding at -0.0082%. Negative funding in perpetual futures markets means that short sellers are paying long sellers to keep positions open, a sign traders are bearish on the market.

XRP fell for 10 straight days from November 5 to November 14, 2026. Price lost momentum after losing the daily MA 200 at the close of October. The cryptocurrency lost another crucial support in early October, the daily MA 50, weakening bulls.

Stablecoin supply is shrinking fast, dropping $17 billion over the last month, the steepest drop since 2022. As holders swap stablecoins to fiat, they drain buying power from BTC and crypto, analysts at Glassnode said.

It will be watched if buying power returns to the market; according to Bitwise CEO Matt Hougan, "There will be a big buying opportunity at some point, but it's hard to say when."

The daily RSI has entered oversold levels, with the possibility of a relief rally in the coming session. Such a recovery, if sustained, will target the daily MA 50 and 200 at $1.25 and $1.30. Further downside is expected to find support at $0.77.

#5169. Hyperliquid Open Interest Surges as Perpetuals Rival Large Exchanges

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Hyperliquid's derivatives business has grown large enough to challenge some familiar centralised exchange names on a key measure: open interest. A third-quarter research report put positions open on the platform at approximately $16.8 billion at the end of September, a major increase over the previous quarter. For a decentralised trading venue, the scale is striking. But comparing it with conventional exchanges requires more than ranking a single number.

Open interest is the value of contracts that remain outstanding, not the amount of cash a venue has collected in fees or the value of trades settled that day. It reveals participation and leverage, while leaving important questions about market depth and resilience unanswered.

The Block's report on Hyperliquid's Q3 figures described open interest reaching roughly $16.8 billion, nearly 80% above the level recorded at the end of June. The report compared that snapshot with data for exchanges including Bybit, Kraken and OKX, though rankings depend on which products, venues and measurement conventions are included.

The headline number suggests that traders are comfortable maintaining substantial derivative exposure on Hyperliquid. It does not prove that all of that exposure is unhedged directional speculation. Some participants may be market makers or arbitrageurs carrying positions that offset risk elsewhere, while other traders could be using leverage to express very concentrated views.

The same Q3 analysis estimated approximately $191 million in protocol revenue, an increase of about 21.7% from the second quarter but a decrease of roughly 36.5% compared with the year-earlier period. That contrast is a useful warning against assuming that more open contracts automatically translate into stronger year-on-year economics. Revenue depends on trading activity, fees and the mix of products used.

Stock-linked perpetual products were another standout, with reported quarterly trading volume of about $153.8 billion, up approximately 161% sequentially. Yet volume can be affected by incentives, volatility and the churn of high-frequency participants. Healthy trading infrastructure also needs reliable pricing, liquid order books and a credible process for handling extreme market moves.

Perpetual futures let traders maintain leveraged exposure without a fixed expiry date. That flexibility comes with funding payments, margin requirements and liquidation risk. During a sharp market decline, forced closing of positions can magnify volatility. A platform's decentralised components do not eliminate the economic risk of carrying leverage or the operational need for robust safeguards.

This is particularly relevant after the week's crypto sell-off. Market watchers should compare open interest with trading volume, funding costs and liquidation activity rather than treating rising positions as inherently bullish. A platform can be technologically impressive and still host dangerous levels of speculative risk.

The rise of on-chain perpetuals adds to the broader discussion about where exchange activity should take place. NewsBTC's coverage of institutional interest in DeFi through Aave shows that traditional crypto firms are exploring decentralised infrastructure. Meanwhile, Aave's reserve-factor debate illustrates the governance decisions that shape the economics of on-chain services.

Hyperliquid's Q3 performance is evidence that decentralised derivatives can operate at considerable scale. The harder test comes during severe volatility, when users will judge it on execution quality, orderly liquidations and transparent risk management. Those outcomes will say more about the durability of its position than any single exchange league table.

#5168. Ripple Prime CEO Steps Down Ahead Of XRP Expansion

Home » Ripple (XRP) » Ripple Prime CEO Steps Down Ahead Of XRP Expansion

XRP’s financial utility goes way beyond cross-border operations: leadership shift shakes Ripple’s prominent brokerage arm.

Ripple’s Ripple Prime institutional crypto brokerage platform is witnessing a major change. Ripple Prime founder Marc Asch is going to leave the position at year-end as Noel Kimmel assumes leadership.

Noel Kimmel, already serving as Vice President of Ripple Prime, will assume leadership of the business and their team within Ripple Labs. This marks Ripple Prime’s transition from a regular custody & brokerage platform to major-scale financial services & infrastructure provider.

Marc Asch founded Hidden Road back in 2018, instantly becoming the growing brokerage’s CEO. The company offered a wide variety of digital-asset powered services, including foreign exchange, derivatives and fixed income. Ripple made a deal to acquire the platform in 2025.

Since the massive acquisition, Ripple Labs expanded the newly-acquired subsidiary’s business towards leveraged ETF financing. The branch even processed $13 trillion in payments during 2025, but the vague regulations kept all of that trading action outside of crypto’s boundaries.

Ripple Prime founder Marc Asch is stepping down at year end, per CoinDesk, with Noel Kimmel taking over the prime brokerage Ripple bought for $1.25B in 2025. Founders leaving a year after a sale is normal. The test is whether institutions keep growing there. Red flag or nothing?

Right now, Ripple Prime serves over 300 institutions worldwide. The freshly-appointed CEO Noel Kimmel was already deeply involved in this expansion. He previously represented Ripple Prime in its $200 million financing agreement, designed to raise institutional margin capacity.

The brokerage platform went on to introduce equity-linked derivatives and financing for Ripple’s institutional customers. Another key milestone was the deal with Brevan Howard, an accounting company looking after $35 billion in assets under management (AUM) for clearing services.

Presently, XRP coin’s trading dangerously close to the psychological $1 level on November 13, 2026. This extends a sharp dip as Bitcoin (BTC) falls below $68,000 and with the crypto ETF market taking a multi-billion dollar hit on Friday. The $0.99 support zone comes into play now.

#5167. 2026 was supposed to be the year of altcoins – So what went wrong?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

For years, investors in the crypto market have followed a familiar pattern. The moment they see that Bitcoin’s price gains are eventually slowing down, they begin moving capital into Ethereum and other altcoins in search of higher returns.

This rotation has historically fueled what the market calls an “altcoin season.”

However, 2026 is beginning to challenge that expectation. Though the market has seen brief periods of outperformance, these have failed to develop into a sustained rally across the wider altcoin ecosystem.

What makes this particularly concerning is that the year is now approaching its end without a convincing sign of the long-awaited rotation.

To understand the case better first, let’s focus on the total altcoin market capitalization, which fell from approximately $1.85 trillion to $1.06 trillion in just a month, erasing almost around $800 billion in value.

At the same time, the Altcoin Season Index has struggled to maintain readings above the 75 threshold, which traditionally signals an altcoin season.

At press time, too, the index was sitting at 48, which means that the Bitcoin [BTC] season continues to be in full swing at the moment despite BTC trading at $73,836.99 at press time.

This came after a recent report by AMBCrypto, which indicated that the median altcoin’s returns by November had dropped slightly below Bitcoin and that both Bitcoin’s 60-day returns and the altcoin’s returns had returned to zero.

Going back in time, the years 2021 and 2023 delivered substantial rallies during different parts of the year. However, the year 2022 remained dominated by bearish sentiment. Moving forward, the end of 2024 is what provides an important point of comparison.

Following Bitcoin’s move above $100,000 in December, market attention expanded towards Ethereum [ETH], XRP, Solana, and other large-cap altcoins. And the broader market entered 2025 with considerable optimism around a potential continuation of that rotation.

However, the experience of 2025 also demonstrated that such rallies are not necessarily sustained. Glassnode’s historical analysis highlighted that altcoin trading volume had previously surged to nearly four times Bitcoin’s volume around September 2025, before cooling as Bitcoin approached its October market top.

The same pattern appeared in September 2026. Altcoin spot volume again climbed to almost four times Bitcoin’s volume, suggesting a period of heightened speculation. By late October, however, the ratio had declined to approximately three times Bitcoin’s volume.

He warned that major altcoins are approaching critical support levels. For instance, Hyperliquid [HYPE] could fall to $64 or $53 if it breaks below $75, while Ondo [ONDO] risks dropping to $0.26 or $0.19 below $0.35. Quant [QNT] could also decline towards $160 if it breaks below $190.

These setups suggest that selling pressure is spreading beyond smaller tokens, making a broad-based altcoin recovery increasingly unlikely in 2026.

Echoing a similar sentiment, another crypto educator on X added,

Using CryptoQuant analysis, he suggested that altcoins have lost approximately $200 billion in market capitalization. Around 70% of Binance-listed altcoins are trading below their 200-day moving averages, while only 13.2% of the top 250 crypto assets recorded positive returns in October.

Then, one of the strongest arguments against an imminent altcoin season is Bitcoin’s continued dominance. At press time too, Bitcoin dominance was around 60.98%.

That said, execs in the crypto communities are also increasingly questioning whether the traditional altcoin cycle is still relevant.

For instance, Benjamin Cowen, founder & CEO of ITC, in his recent commentary, added to this concern, where he argued that the arrival of Q4 highlighted the weakness across the market, with altcoins facing greater pressure.

Unfortunately, with the cryptocurrency market witnessing approximately $738.92 million in liquidations over the past 24 hours, affecting 120,474 traders according to CoinGlass data, concerns are valid.

This is also because the largest altcoin, Ethereum, recorded the highest liquidations at $261.27 million, followed by Bitcoin at $204.45 million. Additionally, Solana [SOL] and Zcash also saw significant liquidations of $38.93 million and $39.47 million, respectively.

Then there is another caveat to figure out, and that is where investors are directing their money.

Well, in the month of November so far, BTC ETFs recorded the largest outflow worth approximately $1.58 billion, followed by ETH ETFs at $551.10 million. Among altcoins, HYPE ETFs saw $18.25 million in outflows, XRP ETFs recorded $18.57 million, while Hedera [HBAR] ETFs registered $2.83 million.

Chainlink [LINK], Avalanche [AVAX], Litecoin [LTC], and Binance [BNB] also recorded smaller outflows. This suggests that investors were withdrawing capital across both Bitcoin and altcoins rather than rotating into smaller cryptocurrencies.

At the same time, the Crypto Fear and Greed Index was standing at ‘Fear’ at 29 at press time. These indicators reinforce one another and suggest that the traditional altcoin season is losing its momentum.

#5166. Bitcoin Rebounds 5.5% Off $62,000 Low as Satoshi Ally Adam Back Sees ‘Bouncy’ Strength

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Blockstream CEO Adam Back has commented on the rebound of Bitcoin after its overnight drop to a four-month low. On Sunday, Back posted a Bitfinex chart of Bitcoin on X with a one-word caption: "Bouncy."

Back created Hashcash, the proof-of-work scheme that Satoshi Nakamoto cited in the original Bitcoin whitepaper, and he was one of the first people Satoshi contacted about Bitcoin.

Early Sunday, the price of Bitcoin fell to $62,029 on Bitfinex, a level it had not seen since July. After that, Bitcoin went up by 5.5%, and the chart shared by Back shows the price at $65,487.

Bouncy. 🟩+5.5% overnight👍 https://t.co/fzspLHXcbr

Bitcoin has lost around 20% over the past month, according to price data by TradingView. Its all-time high of $126,000 was set in October 2025, and Bitcoin is now trading at about half of that level.

Earlier this month, Bitcoin fell below its 200-day moving average near $73,000. On Nov. 13, crypto exchanges liquidated $1.65 billion in 24 hours, and most of the liquidated positions were longs, according to CoinGlass. Besides, investors withdrew almost $3 billion from U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs over the past few weeks, according to SoSoValue.

The decline comes after Binance lost $6.4 billion in a hack in October. Fundstrat managing partner Tom Lee has also called the recent drawdown a rotation of money into mega-IPOs, and Anthropic is set to start trading on Nasdaq on Nov. 19.

On Nov. 12, Back told CNBC that he still expects Bitcoin to reach $1 million in the current cycle. "Personally I have a bet on Bitcoin to top a million by the end of the cycle, which is later in 2028," Back said.

According to Back, around 95% of the capital managed by large asset managers, mutual funds and model portfolios has not yet entered Bitcoin. "We're still 95% or more of that to come in the following years," he said.

Back calls Bitcoin "better gold" because of its capped supply and simple verification. He expects banks and pension funds to become the third wave of demand for Bitcoin, and Vanguard has already allowed its clients to trade crypto ETFs.

"I think we're in the early innings of a bull market cycle," Back added.

#5165. Anchorage Digital’s Stablecoins Can Now Settle Payments on Visa’s Network

Anchorage Digital’s Stablecoins Can Now Settle Payments on Visa’s Network

Anchorage Digital is extending its stablecoin reach into one of the world’s largest payment networks, teaming up with Visa to let select Anchorage-issued tokens settle transactions directly on VisaNet, starting with Western Union’s USDPT.

Under the arrangement announced Thursday, Visa and eligible companies can now use USDPT, a dollar-pegged stablecoin issued and custodied by Anchorage Digital Bank, to settle their obligations on the Visa network.

It’s the first Anchorage-issued token to gain that status, and the companies said the deal establishes a framework to add more of its stablecoins later.

The appeal, Anchorage argues, is pairing the speed of a stablecoin with the backing of a regulated bank. Anchorage operates what it bills as America’s first federally chartered crypto bank, overseen by the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency.

“Speed alone won’t bring institutions onchain,” said Nathan McCauley, Anchorage’s co-founder and CEO, arguing that institutions want “a regulated institution behind the money,” now getting both a stablecoin’s speed and a federally regulated bank’s safeguards.

The move is the latest sign of Visa weaving stablecoins into its settlement plumbing.

The payments giant has built out a stablecoin settlement program spanning networks like Base, Polygon and others, launched a platform to help banks and fintech firms issue and move stablecoins, and has moved into onchain lending tied to its cards.

Dan Roesbery, a Visa vice president for crypto partnerships, framed the USDPT support as giving partners “another trusted, institution-grade option” for settling with Visa.

For Anchorage, the deal advances a broader ambition to become the settlement bank for stablecoins at institutional scale, not just issuing them but providing the custody and settlement rails underneath.

The bank already issues a roster of institution-grade stablecoins, including Tether’s U.S.-regulated USAT, Western Union’s USDPT, Ethena’s USDtb and others, each redeemable one-to-one for dollars. Its counterparty settlement network, Atlas, handles off-exchange settlement, collateral management and cross-border transfers.

Anchorage also recently positioned itself as the custody backbone of the U.S.-Mexico stablecoin corridor, custodying tokens on both sides of one of the world’s largest remittance routes. Backing a token on Visa’s network, which reaches merchants and banks worldwide, extends that institutional footprint further into mainstream payments.

The companies said support for additional Anchorage-issued stablecoins could follow the initial USDPT launch.

#5164. Kraken Parent Payward Reports Quarterly Recovery Despite Weak Year-on-Year Profit

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Kraken parent Payward has reported a sequential improvement in quarterly profitability even as its business remains smaller on several measures than it was a year earlier. The company posted $60 million in adjusted EBITDA for the third quarter of 2026, up from $23 million in the previous quarter, alongside an expansion of its platform beyond conventional cryptocurrency trading.

Those results point to a business trying to diversify while navigating a difficult trading environment. They also require attention to the company's chosen metrics: adjusted EBITDA and its newly emphasised Total Platform Margin are not the same thing as statutory net profit or ordinary gross revenue.

In its November 13 financial update, Payward reported Total Platform Margin, or TPM, of $487 million, compared with $436 million in the second quarter. That represented 12% sequential growth, although TPM was also down 12% from a year earlier. Adjusted EBITDA increased to $60 million from $23 million in Q2, while remaining substantially below the year-earlier result.

Payward has changed how it presents the business, replacing a previously highlighted adjusted-revenue measure with TPM. The company says this approach deducts costs passed on to customers, including certain staking rewards, to provide a better view of what the platform retains. Investors should compare periods on consistent definitions rather than treating a renamed headline metric as automatically equivalent to prior revenue figures.

Total platform transaction volume reached about $353 billion, up 22% quarter on quarter, though down 30% from Q3 2025. Funded accounts increased to around 7.1 million, reflecting an expanding account base even as overall trading conditions remained challenging. The combination suggests growth in the number of customers does not necessarily translate into a proportional increase in transaction activity every quarter.

The company also highlighted transaction-based, asset-based and access-based sources of platform margin. Together, asset and access activity represented slightly more than half of TPM, indicating an effort to rely less exclusively on trading fees. Whether those businesses remain resilient through another difficult market cycle will be more telling than one period of sequential recovery.

Payward's broader ambitions include banking, asset management, services for other businesses and AI-driven financial tools. Its statement describes plans to introduce an agent-oriented layer intended to let customers authorise software to perform actions within programmed risk limits. Such a system raises practical questions about consent, error handling and how a customer reverses an unwanted automated action.

NewsBTC previously covered Payward's proposed move into European banking through Saldo, which illustrates how the company is seeking a wider relationship with customers than a trading account alone. These plans should not be confused with proof that every announced product is already commercially available or licensed in every market.

Expanding into adjacent activities can smooth the effects of volatile crypto trading, but it can also introduce new costs and regulatory obligations. Payward's investment in Aave covered by NewsBTC is another example of the company's interest in market infrastructure beyond centralised exchange transactions.

The next quarterly update should show whether TPM growth continues without depending on a short-lived jump in volumes, and how much of the improvement reaches the bottom line. For now, the Q3 release offers a mixed but informative picture: recovery from Q2, weaker comparisons with last year and a company making a deliberate bet on diversification.

#5163. What’s keeping Bitcoin buyers away despite 2 leverage flushes in 5 days?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

On 29th October, the largest hack in crypto’s history took Bitcoin to a low near $76,200. A few days later, on 5th November, an AI-driven tech crash spilled into crypto. The Nasdaq 100 fell by 6.4%, while semiconductor stocks tumbled by 12%.

This chaos set off leveraged liquidations of roughly $1.1 billion and pushed Bitcoin’s price below $74,000.

Two different shocks, a historical Binance breach, and an AI token sell-off. Though they seem unrelated on the surface, this time they were actually the catalyst for the same event. What Bitcoin saw was a surge in speculative positioning, but what the king coin did not see was a buyer.

The market’s first take on the Binance hack was that crypto had passed a test. Bitcoin fell by about 6% from its 28th October close — a fraction of the 15% to 39% drawdowns after the controversies surrounding Celsius, FTX, BlockFi, and Bybit.

Matt Mena, Crypto Research Strategist at 21Shares, told AMBCrypto,

What we find most encouraging is how the market handled the worst of it. The largest hack in crypto’s history caused a far smaller drawdown than past crises; the protocols kept running, and none of the structural drivers broke.

Unfortunately, institutional resilience is not retail demand. The market simply treated a smaller slump as evidence of Bitcoin’s underlying strength. However, whether anyone wanted to buy Bitcoin [BTC] was a separate question, one the hack never answered.

Still, institutional strength hasn’t eased the feeling of dread felt by many retail investors. And, a major factor can explain why.

The most important signal is what happened after the liquidations: Nothing. Bitget Wallet’s analysts put it plainly that the leverage reset “has cleared some speculative excess, but is yet to address the underlying demand problem.”

Looking at the data, one can see why. Most importantly, the ETF flows changed drastically. There were $2.6 billion worth of inflows in September 2026, which turned into $290 million of net outflows in October. Currently, U.S ETF holders sit underwater below their cost basis of $82K.

How ETF holders behave below breakeven remains the key tell for November.

Also, stablecoin supply has not been moving outside the $305 billion zone, a sure sign that liquidity is sitting still.

Finally, one cannot forget the fallout caused by the Binance hack.

When the exchange reopened withdrawals, the queue moved fast. The exchange processed $4.65 billion against $1.01 billion of deposits in under ten hours; net outflows reached about $5.8 billion over 24 hours!

Each of these factors may not matter when seen separately, but when read together, they describe the same thing — capital leaving or sitting still. The leverage is gone, and the bid is not there to replace it.

For 21Shares, $73,000 is the 200-day moving average, and “the line that would reopen the bear-market debate.”

Bitget analysts told AMBCrypto that Bitcoin must hold the $73K–$74K range.

The 73,000-74,000 range is now the zone Bitcoin needs to hold. Stabilization would require more balanced derivatives positioning and sustained positive ETF flows. Reclaiming and holding $80,000 would signal stronger demand, while a decisive break below $73,000 could expose $68,000. If $68,000 also fails to hold, the selloff would suggest more than a temporary leverage flush.

When the same number pops up again and again, it stops being a coincidence and starts pointing to a pattern — that Bitcoin is either in a temporary flush or at the start of something deeper.

If buyers do return, Bitcoin can comfortably start holding $80,000, and stronger demand would emerge as a result. Getting back above $81,000 to $82,000 would finally put the largest holder cohorts back in profit.

Both reports are similarly aligned on what won’t fix Bitcoin’s buyer problem: Macro relief without demand. In fact, Bitget’s analysts asserted,

A durable Bitcoin recovery will depend on improving liquidity and Spot demand, not macro relief alone.

Matt Mena framed the same requirement as a four-part checklist.

We are watching four things into November: Bitcoin holding $76,000 to $78,000 and reclaiming $80,000 to $82,000, Binance completing withdrawals and proving its reserves, credit spreads, oil and long-end yields, and whether ETF flows return. If those turn, the setup for the final weeks of the year remains constructive.

Notice what’s on both lists: ETF flows, liquidity, Spot demand, and positioning. All of them are things buyers have to do.

There is naturally no doubt that macro factors can move Bitcoin’s price. If everything aligns, a rally can happen tomorrow. However, seasoned investors know that this will never be the same as rebuilding buyer interest.

Ultimately, a macro relief rally would leave Bitcoin’s demand problem exactly where it was.

The calendar offers a boost for Bitcoin regardless of which way the ball drops, as December’s Fed expectations hinge on U.S CPI and PPI readings. For the uninitiated, hotter readings will reinforce rate-hike expectations, and a softer core inflation will trigger a relief rally.

However, Bitget’s analysts draw a line between the two outcomes, noting,

This week’s inflation data adds another test. Hotter readings could reinforce December rate-hike expectations, while softer core inflation could trigger a relief rally. But a durable Bitcoin recovery will depend on improving liquidity and spot demand, not macro relief alone.

Basically, a relief rally can be classed as a macro event, not a demand event. The inflation print can only deliver the former, and the latter can only come about by real flows.

Here, it’s also worth being precise about what the 5th November AI crash tells us. 21Shares calls it,

A renewed wobble in artificial intelligence (AI) equities pulled the Nasdaq-100 down almost 4%.

That’s the mechanism in a nutshell. Even this time, crypto’s fall was the result of trading alongside a risk asset.

Still, if Bitcoin’s move below $74,000 was driven by the AI wipeout, then the absence of a bid is more telling. Eventually, we come back to the same problem of missing buyers.

Leverage has been reset twice in a week — Once by Binance’s $6.3 billion hack, once by an AI shocker. And the buyer bid has hardly appeared either time.

That’s what makes $73,000 a critical level to watch. If buyers return and hold this level, they could help Bitcoin reclaim $80,000. This would also imply that BTC’s demand problem is cyclical, not permanent.

However, a break below $73,000 will hint at — and a failure at $68,000 will confirm — that Bitcoin’s breakdown is structural. Bitcoin’s recovery hinges on a single level, and the lack of buyers has never been felt stronger.

Cryptocurrencies are of particular interest to Saman, with much of her writing centered around understanding how ideas like Momentum and Greater Fool theories apply to altcoins, specifically, memecoins.

#5162. 'Best Reason to Exist': Dogecoin Creator Defends Bitcoin’s Role as 401(k) Alternative

Dogecoin co-creator Billy Markus, who goes by Shibetoshi Nakamoto on X, has weighed in on Bitcoin's potential role as an alternative to traditional retirement savings, describing Bitcoin as having the "best reason to exist."

A 401(k) is an employer-sponsored retirement savings plan in the United States that typically allows workers to invest through a selection of financial products, often including mutual funds and exchange-traded funds.

The discussion began on X as market participants reacted to the ongoing market crash, which has seen millions of dollars in liquidations in the last 24 hours. Leveraged traders took the brunt, with $1.92 billion of liquidations over 24 hours, according to CoinGlass data. Longs accounted for the majority of the damage at $1.59 billion, while short liquidations totaled $341.26 million.

Bitcoin fell to a three-month low below $63,000; Dogecoin fell to a low of $0.055, last seen in June 2023. At the time of writing, Dogecoin was down 4.63% over the last 24 hours to $0.059.

An X user commented, apparently in light of the market drop, "Cash out or crash out."

@mikethree bitcoin has the best reason to exist as the og, ethereum is a massive failure of its own goals but has positive brand recognition, they’re all dumb tokens for degenerates

Dogecoin cofounder Billy Markus responded, saying, "I just sell immediately and hold very little since the market is just a delusion indicator and that’s too random to track."

Another X user joined the discussion, saying Bitcoin remains "a good 401k replacement imo but depends on risk profile obviously." Markus replied to this, saying, "Bitcoin has the best reason to exist as the OG."

As cryptocurrency adoption increases, more traditionally-minded investors have begun exploring digital assets as part of their retirement plans.

Asset manager Fidelity allows Bitcoin in 401(k) plans, depending on employer approval. Fidelity is primarily known for its huge portfolio of retirement accounts and is among the largest 401(k) providers in the U.S. Fidelity also has a standard account option, announced in April 2022, that allows employees with 401(k) accounts to add Bitcoin, in addition to its more traditional accounts.

Fidelity Investments announced a new retirement plan in 2025 allowing U.S. adults to directly invest in cryptocurrencies such as Bitcoin, Ethereum and Litecoin.

#5161. XRP Backer Counters SWIFT’s New Speed Report

Home » Ripple (XRP) » XRP Backer Counters SWIFT’s New Speed Report

The preference for nearly-immediate settlement time windows unites a roster of banks in SWIFT’s chain trial.

A Swift report highlighting faster cross-border payments drew a pointed response from Digital Asset Investor, who replied that he prefers 3-4 seconds and pointed to XRP.

The exchange underscores the ongoing contrast between improvements in traditional interbank messaging and the settlement times associated with blockchain-based rails.

Swift’s Spotlight on Speed report, released this week, shows that 50% of payments on its network now reach the recipient institution within 40 seconds, with 75% arriving within 10 minutes.

What will define the next generation of cross-border payments?The answer? More than just speed.Throughout @Sibos, conversations repeatedly focused on the customer experience. How do we make cross-border transactions more intuitive, more transparent and more predictable for… pic.twitter.com/yZoeZMDN4z

The cooperative noted that these figures put the industry ahead of the G20’s target for one-hour end-to-end processing by 2027 in the interbank leg of the journey.

The same report emphasizes that the “last mile”, the period after a payment reaches the receiving bank and before it is credited to the beneficiary’s account, still accounts for about 80% of the average end-to-end journey time.

Across 40 receiving markets analyzed, median last-mile processing sat near one hour in roughly one-third of cases, with wide variation between cross-border markets.

The conglomerate has called for greater use of one-leg-out models that connect international payments to domestic instant systems to reduce those final delays.

Digital Asset Investor’s reply to the Swift post was brief: “I prefer 3-4 seconds. XRP.” The comment references the XRP Ledger’s typical ledger close time of roughly 3-5 seconds, which provides finality for on-ledger transfers.

I prefer 3-4 seconds. XRP. https://t.co/tBqXtEsqeM

In Ripple’s On-Demand Liquidity (ODL) model, XRP is used as a bridge asset so that currency conversion and settlement can occur without pre-funded nostro accounts.

End-to-end ODL flows that include exchange conversions have been described in the 3-5 second range for the core ledger step, though real-world completion including compliance and local payouts is often measured in seconds to a few minutes depending on the corridor.

The two claims are not measuring identical segments of a payment. Swift’s 40-second figure tracks time to the recipient institution, while the longer last-mile delays occur after that point.

https://t.co/ZA3CcggMOf @swiftcommunity @XRP @realfrandeolza @Archie_XRPL

XRP’s cited 3-4 second window refers primarily to ledger settlement of the bridge asset itself.

Traditional systems still handle the bulk of global bank-to-bank volume and benefit from established compliance and messaging standards; blockchain rails emphasize reduced pre-funding and faster finality on the asset transfer.

Whether the shorter settlement window on XRP produces a meaningfully better end-user experience continues to depend on how the on- and off-ramps, local banking rails, and compliance steps are integrated in any given corridor.

#5160. Twenty One Capital’s $1.09 Billion Profit Is Mostly a Bitcoin Accounting Story

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Twenty One Capital has reported net income of about $1.09 billion for the third quarter, a figure that might look extraordinary for a Bitcoin-focused company. But nearly all of that profit came from changes in the fair value of its Bitcoin holdings rather than cash generated by selling services. That makes the result a useful lesson in how digital-asset treasury accounting can obscure the difference between holding an appreciated asset and operating a profitable business.

Neither figure should be ignored. Bitcoin holdings can create real economic value, but marked-to-market gains can reverse quickly when prices change.

According to The Block's account of Twenty One Capital's filing, the company reported $1.087 billion in quarterly net income, driven largely by around $1.093 billion in fair-value increases on Bitcoin. At the same time, it recorded an operating loss of approximately $4.8 million and interest expense near $1.34 million.

That contrast is the heart of the story. An asset can become more valuable on the balance sheet without generating money to pay salaries or service borrowing costs. Accounting rules may require those changes to appear in reported earnings, but investors still need to separate recurring business performance from market revaluation when assessing sustainability.

Twenty One Capital reported holding 43,514 BTC at September 30, with its holdings valued using a Bitcoin price around $83,700 at the reporting date. The company also disclosed approximately $102.8 million in cash and $486.5 million of convertible-note principal collateralised by a portion of its Bitcoin. Those liabilities and collateral arrangements are important because holding a volatile asset alongside debt creates distinct liquidity and financing considerations.

A higher Bitcoin price can make the balance sheet appear stronger. A lower price can compress asset values while obligations remain, potentially forcing management to reconsider capital allocation. That is why the composition of the treasury, maturity profile and collateral terms can be just as relevant as the headline quantity of BTC.

Chief executive Rapha Zagury has made clear that asset appreciation and profits earned through operations are different. The company has discussed possible opportunities in mining, lending, treasury-related services and share repurchases. Those ideas could broaden its sources of income, but they should be treated as areas under consideration until the business publishes concrete agreements, operating milestones and results.

Other Bitcoin treasury companies face similar questions. NewsBTC's coverage of benchmark treatment for Strategy and Metaplanet highlights how public-market investors consider corporate exposure to Bitcoin through lenses that extend beyond the coin's price.

The same filing showed a net loss for the first nine months of 2026, underscoring how results can swing with changing Bitcoin prices. Investors evaluating a treasury business should consider asset value per share, the effect of any new share or debt issuance, cash needs and whether proposed business lines eventually contribute positive operating cash flow.

There is a market-wide backdrop too. Recent Bitcoin ETF redemptions covered by NewsBTC show that demand for BTC exposure can weaken abruptly. Twenty One Capital's quarterly statement is not evidence that the Bitcoin treasury model has either won or failed. It is evidence that accounting earnings can be dominated by the underlying asset — and that understanding the difference is essential.

#5159. XRP Ledger Passes 11.7 Million Sub-Dollar AI Agent Microtransactions

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Chandler Fang, founder of AI payments startup t54.ai, has said that the company's x402 Facilitator on the XRP Ledger (XRPL) has processed more than 10 million AI agent transactions. Fang made the comments in an interview that the XRPL Foundation published on X on November 15, and the interviewer put the number of these payments at 11.7 million.

According to Fang, XRPL had almost no transactions made by AI agents three or four months before the interview, which was recorded at XRP Seoul 2026. "The entire volume got ramped up very quickly in the three months," he said.

The x402 Facilitator works as a bridge between AI agents and payments, and agents can use XRP or the RLUSD stablecoin to move money without a separate wallet setup for every agent. Fang said that more than 200 merchants are already on the network.

According to Fang, the most common use case is paying for AI compute. Instead of paying $20 to $200 a month for an AI subscription, an agent can pay around $1 or 50 cents for a single task. "Instead, they can purchase this single compute for about $1, or 50 cents, and it can be settled on the XRPL," Fang said.

Fang said that such payments have low value and high frequency, so the high throughput and low fees of XRPL give the network an advantage. He also expects agent payments to move into real-life purchases over the next few quarters. "We have the coffee shop right outside our booth, right? And we are trying to empower users to use XRP and agent payments to finish a real-life transaction," Fang said.

According to Fang, trust is the main issue for agent payments, and t54.ai's Trustline product focuses on risk management for the financial activity of AI agents. He compared the service with the chargeback feature of credit cards. "So there is no lawsuit. It is a similar experience for agents," Fang said.

t54.ai is also part of Mastercard's agentic payment program, where the startup is responsible for risk management. The x402 Facilitator processes transactions based on Mastercard Verifiable Intent, a protocol that verifies the behavior of AI agents.

Fang said that t54.ai, Ripple and Mastercard are in talks about a three-way collaboration, and the first results will be shown at an upcoming hackathon.

"The ten million transactions are just a starting point. I think next year you should expect a lot more transactions. Maybe even more transactions settled on XRPL will be done by AI agents than by humans," Fang said.

#5158. Why Binance’s ‘kill switch’ couldn’t stop hackers from draining $6.3B — Explained!

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

There is a certain irony in pulling the emergency brake, only to discover that the machinery you’re trying to stop is operating beyond your reach.

Binance encountered a rather expensive version of that problem on 29th October. Attackers compromised its wallet signing infrastructure and obtained authority to move cryptocurrency through mechanisms that did not depend on the exchange’s ordinary withdrawal process. Suspending withdrawals could interrupt that process. It could do considerably less about permissions already established on Ethereum.

By the time the attack was contained, approximately $6.3 billion had been stolen and Binance’s engineers had developed a specialised procedure to revoke malicious on-chain authority. The exchange could shut down affected services, but Ethereum would continue recognising the delegation until it was explicitly cleared.

Brian Cubellis, Chief Strategy Officer at Bitcoin custody platform Onramp, offered his two cents to AMBCrypto,

Pausing withdrawals turns off the exchange’s own software, but it does nothing about permissions the attacker has already written to the blockchain

The distinction is an uncomfortable one for an institution entrusted with billions in customer assets.

Binance subsequently coordinated freezes of stolen funds and began replenishing the affected holdings, reporting approximately 98% replenishment by 4th November. Its financial response was substantial.

However, it still is worth questioning what remained possible while its emergency controls were taking effect.

By the way, for those unaware, the attack began well before 29th October and nowhere near a blockchain.

According to Binance’s 3rd November incident report, an engineer downloaded a tampered installation package for an AI coding tool in June. The exchange detected the anomaly and replaced the device, but the attackers had already gathered intelligence about its employees and infrastructure. Well into September, another engineer was approached through a fictitious recruitment process involving interviews and a malicious coding assignment.

By 20th October, the attackers had used compromised employee systems and internal AI tooling to access additional machines. In doing so, they ultimately obtained credentials that opened the way to Binance’s wallet signing infrastructure.

It all paid off on the 29th. Between 20:05 and 20:09 UTC, hot wallets transferred approximately 223.46 million XRP, 970,500 SOL and 44.2 million OP to attacker controlled addresses. Bitcoin and other assets followed. Chainalysis subsequently estimated the overall theft at approximately $6.4 billion.

What was the most consequential part of this intrusion though? The attackers gained access to Binance’s signing infrastructure, the very machinery responsible for authorizing transactions.

On Ethereum, the attackers exploited EIP-7702, a mechanism that allows an externally owned account to delegate execution to smart-contract code.

Ordinarily, that flexibility expands what a wallet can do. Here, according to Binance, the compromised signing infrastructure was used to grant malicious code execution rights over an Ethereum hot wallet. The exchange described 50 signed transactions involved in manipulating the account’s nonce and establishing the delegation.

The unsettling part comes next though. Those permissions remained valid on-chain even after Binance suspended withdrawals and shut down its hot-wallet services. The exchange could stop its own systems from processing transactions, but it could not simply switch off the authority the attackers had already established on Ethereum.

Then came another complication. Binance’s hot wallet used multi-party computation (MPC), which distributes the production of cryptographic signatures across multiple participants or components rather than relying on a single conventional private key. That architecture can reduce certain risks, but it also meant there was no single private key available to revoke the malicious delegation immediately.

Binance’s engineers had to DEVELOP a specialised revocation procedure while the incident was UNFOLDING. Of course, this took additional time, prolonging the effort to contain unauthorized asset movements.

At 23:56 UTC, the final reported unauthorized Ethereum transfer, involving 2,961.9 ETH, was confirmed on-chain. Binance said it had been broadcast earlier and remained pending before confirmation. At 00:10 UTC on 30th October, the exchange cleared the malicious EIP-7702 delegation with a new authorization to the zero address.

The distinction between broadcast and confirmation matters. So does the absence of an established total for funds transferred after withdrawals were suspended.

Binance acknowledged that suspension alone could not stop every unauthorized movement, but its disclosures do not establish a reconciled post-suspension loss figure.

There is a comforting simplicity to the idea of a kill switch. Something goes wrong, someone pulls the plug and the damage stops. Unfortunately, the blockchain has no particular obligation to respect an exchange’s internal emergency procedures.

Consider a hotel discovering that someone has obtained an unauthorized key card. Closing reception prevents new cards from being issued. The existing card still opens the door. Cancelling it, securing the valuables, and recovering what has already disappeared are separate jobs.

Now, this comparison has its limits, but it captures the dilemma facing exchanges whose wallet permissions outlive their own systems.

The problem surfaced in a public exchange between Haseeb Qureshi, Managing Partner at Dragonfly, and  Tudor Anastasiu. Tudor Anastasiu is a blockchain infrastructure and security engineer and the founder of Anastasiu Labs.

The disagreement exposes a distinction that is easy to overlook in the middle of a breach.

A defensive sweep can move assets to safety, provided the exchange can authorize and execute it before the attacker does. However, moving funds does not necessarily invalidate permissions already granted on-chain and it certainly cannot retrieve assets that have already disappeared.

So, that leaves a rather awkward question: If the signing infrastructure itself has been compromised, how does an exchange authorize an emergency transaction without trusting the very system it suspects?

Carlos Vendrell, Founder of Zealynx Security, sees the answer in testing the procedure under exactly those conditions.

The drill should start from a list of every approval and delegation each hot wallet has given, then revoke one of them for real while assuming the normal signing service is the compromised part, and time it

Binance’s experience does not establish that MPC inherently prevents rapid revocation. Nor does its disclosure prove that no emergency procedure existed before the breach.

However, it does establish that engineers developed a specialised procedure during the incident and doing so took additional time.

There was another complication here, one that made the situation look almost absurd. The compromised wallet was reportedly STILL receiving money.

In an analysis dated 4th November, Four Pillars reported that Binance’s deposit-consolidation system continued collecting funds into a compromised Ethereum wallet identified as Binance 14. This allowed the attacker to sweep newly arriving assets.

Nexus Mutual’s reconstruction offers a particularly striking sequence.

At approximately 21:44 UTC, it says the attacker swept 200 million USDT that another Binance-labelled wallet had deposited roughly 72 seconds earlier. Seventy-two seconds! Barely enough time to make a cup of coffee, yet apparently sufficient for a fresh transfer to reach a compromised address and disappear.

The researchers’ findings raise a problem distinct from EIP-7702. An exchange can work to revoke an attacker’s authority while other operational systems continue directing funds towards a dangerous destination. Interrupting those internal asset flows is another containment task and requires its own controls and confirmation.

According to Brian Cubellis, emergency preparedness must account for the loss of the usual signing infrastructure.

An exchange should prove, before it is attacked, that it can cancel any permission on its wallets and move exposed funds to safety even if its own signing system has been taken over

That is easier prescribed than engineered though.

An independent emergency signing path may accelerate recovery, but it also introduces powerful authority that must be secured. Build too much dependence into the ordinary system and the emergency route may fail alongside it. Concentrate too much authority in the alternative and the supposed safeguard becomes another attractive target.

Once stolen assets leave the exchange’s immediate control, containment becomes a race involving investigators, stablecoin issuers, blockchain networks and the attacker.

Chainalysis’s investigation also described an attempt to move approximately $500 million in stablecoins through a swapping address. Within roughly 20 minutes, the attackers reportedly converted around $325 million into assets less susceptible to issuer freezes, including Ether. The remaining $175 million was frozen.

There was a reason for the rush. Stablecoin issuers such as Tether and Circle can freeze certain tokens at designated addresses. Bitcoin and Ether, by contrast, have no equivalent central issuer with that power. Converting the stolen stablecoins into other assets could therefore put them beyond the reach of an issuer-imposed freeze.

This whole episode illustrates the limits of intervention once funds have escaped. Investigators may be able to follow the money on-chain, but knowing where it has gone does not necessarily give them the power to retrieve it.

Binance nevertheless managed to secure a substantial portion of the stolen assets. In its 3rd November report, the exchange said that approximately $2.85 billion had been frozen or intercepted through cooperation with issuers, law enforcement, and blockchain intelligence firms. By 4th November, it reported replenishing approximately 98% of the affected holdings through replacement purchases.

Of course, Binance had demonstrated considerable capacity to absorb the losses rather than pass them on to users. However, it tells us how much the exchange managed to secure or replace, not how effectively it prevented the attacker from continuing to move assets during the breach.

A year earlier, Bybit had encountered a different version of the problem with compromised transaction authority. In February 2025, attackers stole approximately $1.5 billion after compromising the environment associated with its Safe{Wallet} approval process.

According to Bybit’s forensic disclosure, malicious changes deceived signers into approving an unauthorized transaction. It also said that investigators found no indication that its own infrastructure had been compromised.

Both incidents exposed how much depends on the integrity of the authority behind a transaction. Multiple signatures offer limited protection when signers are misled about what they are approving. And as Binance discovered, shutting down a signing service does not necessarily invalidate authority already established on-chain.

An emergency kill switch sounds reassuring. Rather less reassuring is discovering, halfway through a breach, that the attacker has permissions your switch cannot revoke.

Binance’s experience raises an uncomfortable question for exchanges entrusted with billions in customer assets — Have they actually tested what happens when the systems responsible for protecting those assets become part of the attack?

An exchange should be able to demonstrate that it can revoke compromised permissions, move exposed assets to safety, and interrupt internal transfers without relying entirely on infrastructure it can no longer trust.

But what should customers make of an exchange’s public assurances when a breach is underway? AMBCrypto put that question to Vik Sharma, Founder and CEO of Cake Wallet,

A useful update names which approvals were revoked, which assets moved, and where, so customers can check it themselves

There are legitimate reasons to withhold sensitive operational details while an intrusion is underway. Once the immediate danger has passed, however, an exchange should be able to explain what its emergency controls actually stopped, when compromised authority was extinguished, and whether any assets remained exposed.

Binance deserves credit for publishing details of its response, coordinating substantial asset freezes, and committing its own resources to replenish affected holdings. Those measures helped absorb the financial damage. However, they also revealed why reimbursement and containment should never be confused.

A kill switch should buy defenders control, not merely time to figure out where control has gone.

#5157. Thailand Proposes Daily Stablecoin Transfer Cap for Regulated Platforms

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Thailand's Securities and Exchange Commission is seeking feedback on restrictions that could significantly change how customers move stablecoins through regulated digital-asset operators. The proposed rules would generally limit eligible transfers into and out of a customer's account to 5 million baht per day in each direction, while requiring wallets at both ends to belong to that customer.

This is a consultation, not a rule that has already taken effect. The details, exemptions and eventual text matter more than the headline dollar equivalent, especially for businesses using stablecoins for legitimate cross-border payments.

In its November 12 announcement, the Thai SEC said the draft aims to reduce the risk that regulated crypto businesses facilitate money laundering, cybercrime or circumvention of cross-border transfer rules. The proposal would generally require stablecoins entering or leaving a customer's account at a digital-asset operator to come from, or go to, an account or wallet verified as belonging to that same person.

The draft also calls for controls aligned with the Travel Rule, screening for risky wallets and transaction monitoring. Those measures aim to improve traceability where a licensed intermediary is involved. They do not mean all movements of stablecoins across every blockchain would suddenly be visible to, or controlled by, one Thai exchange.

Under the proposal, inbound and outbound transfers would each be limited separately to 5 million baht per customer, per day, with each operator. The SEC's announcement also describes exemptions, including qualifying transfers between the customer's own accounts at regulated operators, certain regulated businesses and approved liquidity-provision activities. The exact scope depends on the final regulation and whether participants satisfy the relevant requirements.

That nuance is easy to lose in a short headline. It would be inaccurate to say Thailand is proposing a universal daily blockchain transfer limit, or that every business must stop moving more than that amount under all circumstances. The consultation is specifically focused on activity conducted through supervised digital-asset operators.

Stablecoins have become useful tools for businesses moving money across borders, particularly where traditional transfers are expensive or slow. NewsBTC's report on Bitso Business's stablecoin payments shows the commercial use case behind this growth. For such companies, wallet verification and transfer limits create compliance work that must fit alongside existing customer checks.

The risk is that rules designed to catch illicit activity could also make ordinary, lawful operations cumbersome if identity matching is difficult or exceptions are unclear. The regulator, meanwhile, has a legitimate interest in ensuring supervised firms know who is using their systems. That tension is precisely what a public consultation is supposed to examine.

The SEC has invited feedback through November 26, 2026. Businesses should review the official draft rather than rely on simplified social-media summaries, particularly when deciding whether their specific transaction flows would be covered. The same distinction between technology and regulated access appears in NewsBTC's coverage of USDC on Solana through Samsung Wallet.

Thailand's proposal reflects a growing regulatory emphasis on the point where blockchain assets interact with licensed businesses. Whether the final rules strike the intended balance will depend on clear exemptions, workable verification and evidence that the controls reduce risk without unnecessarily obstructing legitimate payments.

#5156. Binance Warns About AI Scams

Cryptocurrency exchange Binance has warned of the rising risk of AI scams, including the use of deepfakes that impersonate Binance executives and staff.

The world's largest crypto exchange recently took to X to warn its users that improvements in artificial intelligence are making it harder to distinguish fake messages from real ones.

"AI deepfakes are making impersonation scams harder to spot," the crypto exchange said in a post.

The cryptocurrency exchange has assured its users that no one from Binance will ever reach out to them asking for money or offering unsolicited investment advice.

Binance says scammers are making realistic deepfake videos featuring executives and employees of the company.

Fraudsters are using these deepfakes, some of which are very realistic, to trick potential targets through fraudulent video calls.

That way, people who fall victim to the scam might think they are interacting with real Binance personnel.

Binance warned that scammers often try to reach potential targets through messaging apps such as WhatsApp or Telegram. They impersonate Binance employees by using their names, photos, and job titles to appear credible.

After making contact with a potential victim, the scammer may try to convince them to send crypto, take part in an exclusive investment opportunity, or take other financial actions.

Binance has called on crypto users to be cautious about any unexpected messages they receive, even if they seem to be from someone at the company they know.

A realistic appearance no longer guarantees a person's identity, given that AI can now easily mimic voices and facial expressions.

Users should double-check any suspicious messages through official Binance support channels before taking any action.

#5154. Tron, Tether and Chainalysis Form a New Crypto-Crime Task Force—Replacing One That Just Collapsed

Tron, Tether and Chainalysis Form a New Crypto-Crime Task Force—Replacing One That Just Collapsed

Tron, Tether, and blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis have launched a joint effort to hunt down and disrupt criminal activity involving the USDT stablecoin, just weeks after a nearly identical alliance, with a different analytics partner, quietly fell apart.

Announced Wednesday, the Digital Crime Task Force aims to identify illicit activity involving Tether’s USDT on the Tron blockchain and surface it for law enforcement before criminals can move their funds.

The pitch is a shift from reactive to proactive: rather than waiting for a victim to report a crime or an investigator to flag tainted funds, the group says it will proactively trace criminal networks and coordinate asset freezes, drawing on Chainalysis’s intelligence and AI-powered investigative tools. It will also accept tips and freeze requests from police.

“Criminals are using AI to move faster than ever, and waiting for a victim to report a crime is no longer good enough,” said Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino, noting the company has frozen more than $7 billion tied to illicit activity while working with over 360 law enforcement agencies across 73 countries.

Tron founder Justin Sun framed it as protecting the millions who rely on USDT, which circulates on Tron to the tune of more than $94 billion.

The notable wrinkle is what this new alliance replaces. Tron and Tether previously ran a similar operation, the T3 Financial Crime Unit, formed in 2024 with rival analytics firm TRM Labs and later joined by Binance. Together, the group froze more than $450 million in tainted assets.

But Bloomberg reported earlier this month that T3 was quietly disbanded in late August, with people familiar citing slow response times and a lack of coordination among its members. Its collapse went public just days before the record $6.4 billion Binance hack.

Swapping TRM for Chainalysis is a pointed move, given the two are fierce competitors. Chainalysis has even sued the U.S. government over an ICE contract awarded to TRM without competitive bidding, underscoring the rivalry now running through crypto’s crime-fighting infrastructure.

The new task force says it will target a broad sweep of threats, from romance and investment scams to stolen funds, terrorist financing, North Korean cyber operations, cartel money laundering and child-exploitation material.

The group also framed the effort around a coming era of autonomous AI agents transacting onchain, which it warned will open fresh avenues for criminal exploitation.

Solana is the most vulnerable, trading at $71.89 below all major moving averages, with the $60 level as the closest support.

Zcash looks stronger, holding the 200 EMA at $915 after a 6% daily gain, though it needs to reclaim $1,000 to avoid a deeper correction. Shiba Inu has lost its July low of $0.00000413 and trades near $0.00000391 with RSI around 25, so a short-term retrace is possible, but bears on the market prevail

Shiba Inu has been struggling on the market for ages, and November 14 made things worse: SHIB broke below its July low of $0.00000413 and dipped to around $0.0000037.

SHIB mostly followed the uptrend from July to October, but tumbled quickly due to the general reversal of the cryptocurrency market. The bounce from the July level was unlikely to occur naturally, and the zone where some buy orders might have been concentrated didn't hold.

Back in September, the 200 EMA acted as strong resistance that SHIB was unable to break, and the asset has been sliding ever since. The ascending trendline from July was lost back in October.

SHIB is trading at $0.00000391, bouncing slightly from the low. The drop below $0.000004 isn't a guarantee of anything, as SHIB has already been lower in previous years. RSI is moving around 25, deep in the oversold zone. Multiple indicators suggest a retrace, but the overall market picture is grim, to say the least.

According to CryptoQuant, SHIB held on exchanges is worth around $332 million, while the netflow stays negative. In tokens, around 86 trillion SHIB still sit on exchanges. Most of the activity is still most likely fueled by retail investors, as the largest players stay sidelined or are not exposed to SHIB at all.

Shiba Inu volumes are still low, which suggests a lack of volatility ahead. The market is weak, and it might get weaker, though most of the selling pressure SHIB could face has most likely been injected already.

Solana is clearly struggling, and the death cross on the chart is not likely to help it push higher. The volume uptick reinforces the bearish narrative and could push the price even lower. The volume on November 14 was the highest since early September.

SOL is trading at $71.89 after dipping to $67.37, around 42% below its September peak. The correction is rapidly erasing the rally Solana has built since August, and the price is already back in the range last seen in July.

Solana trades below all major moving averages, with most of them pointing down, which signals that the current trend is getting worse. RSI is beyond oversold, and it is not clear where the selling pressure is coming from. We also saw additional injections of capital from whales, so the current downtrend is mostly fueled by capitulation of larger players, though not by institutions.

The death cross we are witnessing on the EMAs is important, but barely significant for the asset itself. It is a mere reflection of market sentiment, rather than anything else. The short-term moving average is falling rapidly, which suggests an extremely negative short-term dynamic.

The blue moving average crossed below the black one at around $96, roughly 25% above the current price. Realistically, the closest "fair" support is the $60 price level, where Solana bounced back in June.

A better time to buy Solana could be tied to an overall market recovery, which needs a stronger market catalyst that does not exist at this point. Unfortunately, there are little to no fundamental factors that can help Solana reach key market levels right now.

Zcash rapidly dived toward the 200 EMA, and the downtrend isn't going anywhere. However, the situation is very different from Solana. A solid support still exists for the price, and the situation is not as dire.

Zcash has a strong foundation, which acts as a bouncing pad for the asset, even though it lost 46% from the local top, when it traded close to $1,700. ZEC is trading at $915, up almost 6% today after a wick toward $800 on November 14. The longest moving average is still moving below the price, suggesting the long-term uptrend isn't broken yet.

The NU7 update, activated on November 6, also made the Zcash network somewhat healthier by decreasing the target block interval to 25 seconds.

The price violated the $1,000 level, and the moving averages are trying to converge rapidly. The convergence of moving averages might lead to a volatility spike. From the momentum perspective, RSI is close to 30, so the asset is almost oversold.

In order to avoid a deeper correction, Zcash must reclaim the $1,000 price level. If the price violates the 200 EMA, our next stop would be the $800 area, which acts as a psychological threshold rather than a strong technical level.

#5150. With Bitcoin near $65K, can history repeat BTC’s March 2023 comeback?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The U.S. headline CPI (Consumer Price Index) was up 0.4% in October, compared to a 0.3% expectation. The metric’s brisk rise for the third straight month signalled that underlying inflation pressure was building up.

Inflation pressures came from high oil prices (due to the conflict with Iran) and import tariffs, alongside robust household and business spending.

On Tuesday, the 10th of November, Fed Governor Christopher Waller raised the prospect of an interest rate hike of 50bps.

Unlike the previous Federal Open Market Committee (FOMC) meeting, there is no debate now about whether the Fed should pause or hike, but rather whether the December meeting will result in a 25 bps or 50 bps rate hike.

As a risk-on asset, Bitcoin and the rest of the crypto market are likely to suffer reduced capital inflows and lower demand. Retail pessimism was high, and there is debate around whether it is a good time to buy or still too risky.

The loss of the $80k level towards the end of October, alongside negative apparent demand, was a warning sign of further losses. Shark accumulation while the leading crypto’s price held above $75k was hopeful, but ultimately the sellers won the battle.

As things stand, the strong downward move could also be part of a leverage purge.

Within just three months, the price has retraced into a key demand zone on the weekly chart. This timeframe’s bias remains bullish, and the pullback is still healthy. A session close below $64,133, the 78.6% Fibonacci retracement level of the swing move higher to $87,395, would be a sign that the bullish woes were about to extend.

The 6-month liquidation heatmap showed that clusters of liquidity built up around $74k and $68k have been taken out. The next major liquidity pocket to watch was $61.7k, with $64k-$65k also a region to watch.

Price is likely to be pulled lower to these areas in the coming days or weeks.

The realized price of different holder cohorts above showed that the short-term holders were mostly underwater. The 3-6 month cohort has its realized price at $65k, marking an important on-chain support level.

This level has a confluence with the liquidation heatmap, as well as the weekly imbalance. Hence, swing traders and investors have to be prepared for the possibility of a retest of this realized price level.

The adjusted MVRV metric employs both the 30-day and 365-day MVRV ratios to capture short-term capital inflows and momentum, as well as mark out the macro cost basis trend.

The aMVRV was above the baseline to signal a bull market phase crossover. Hence, the Bitcoin market regime has passed early bull territory and is in a bullish regime.

The deep price pullback in recent weeks was similar to the price pullback from $25,250 to $19,549 in March 2023. Back then, the market remained within a bullish phase despite the deep setback.

The bearish market pressure should not be taken lightly, data shows. At the same time, Bitcoin has a chance of recovery if it can hold above $64k-$65k.

This hold will likely look like a consolidation phase, followed by easing loss-selling and short-term holder panic. If these conditions are met, and others such as ETF capital inflows to signal sentiment recovery come true, investors can have more faith in a return to a bull market.

#5149. US Seeks to Seize More Than $1.2 Billion Linked to Binance Hack

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

US authorities say they have obtained court approval to seize or restrain more than $1.2 billion in digital assets linked to the major October attack on Binance. The action is part of a wider effort to trace and freeze funds attributed to North Korean hackers, and it comes alongside a separate disruption of infrastructure allegedly used to deceive victims through fake video calls.

The distinction between freezing assets, receiving permission to seize them and actually returning money to victims is crucial. A large enforcement announcement should not be presented as evidence that all the stolen cryptocurrency has already been recovered.

The Block reported on the Department of Justice announcement that court approval had been secured to seize or restrain more than $1.2 billion tied to the October 29 Binance breach. US authorities have attributed the attack to North Korean operators associated with the TraderTraitor group, with the initial theft described as exceeding $6 billion.

The department said more than $2.7 billion had been frozen through the combined efforts of authorities and private-sector companies. A frozen balance is not the same as funds delivered back to an exchange or its customers. Investigators must still establish ownership, navigate legal processes and prevent assets from being moved through other services or jurisdictions.

Public blockchain records can show a trail of transfers from one address to another. That visibility can be valuable when investigators identify wallets and work with trading platforms or other service providers to block proceeds. But hackers can use bridges, mixers and chains with different transaction models to complicate tracing. Technical identification also has to be connected to a legal basis for seizure.

Another challenge is timing. An asset may be visible on-chain but move before a service can act, or it may already have been exchanged into another token. That is why seizure orders, freezes and completed recoveries need to be reported separately. The value of assets at the time of the theft can also differ from what can be recovered months later.

The DOJ also described action against a scheme referred to as FaceStealer, which allegedly used manipulated or impersonated video calls to compromise victims. Reporting said the operation had been associated with more than $400 million in theft over an 18-month period. Although both announcements involve North Korean cyber activity, they are distinct investigations and should not be merged into a single total of stolen funds.

The underlying lesson is operational. Attackers are increasingly interested in persuading a person to compromise their own device or sign an unsafe transaction, not merely in exploiting smart-contract code. Wallet permissions, staff identity verification and secure approval procedures can therefore matter as much as blockchain analytics after the fact.

Binance has said it arranged coverage for affected customer assets, according to the reporting, but that statement should be assessed separately from the government's legal recovery process. Customers should rely on official exchange communications for account-specific details, and be cautious of unsolicited messages offering compensation or recovery services. Such approaches can become a second opportunity for fraud.

The broader industry is paying attention to withdrawal approval controls and custody practices. NewsBTC's coverage of business Bitcoin accounts with withdrawal approvals and institutional interest in DeFi infrastructure highlight parts of that security conversation. The DOJ action is meaningful, but a final recovery figure will only be clear as investigations and legal proceedings progress.
